Theorem D.3 (Soundness of Algorithmic Type Assignment).

For all
\Gamma,\Delta
, if
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma)\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}\Delta
then
\Gamma\cup C\vdash\Delta
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the derivation of
\vdash_{2}
.

(Inst2)
:

This case is trivial by the use of
(GVar)
.

(Var2)
:

Let
\Gamma,\,x:A
be the context with
A\sqsubseteq B
. By
(Id)
, we have
\Gamma,\,x:A\vdash x:A
. By
(SubR)
, we have
\Gamma,\,x:A\vdash x:B
as required.

(FixsR2)
:

Let
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma)\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}(\mathsf{fix}\,f(x).\,M):A
and
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma),\,f:A,\,x:B_{1}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}M:B_{2}
with
\Gamma\vdash B_{1}\to B_{2}\sqsubseteq A
.

By the inductive hypothesis,
\Gamma,\,f:A,\,x:B_{1}\vdash M:B_{2}
.

By
(SubL)
we have
\Gamma,\,f:B_{1}\to B_{2},\,x:B_{1}\vdash M:B_{2}
.

By
(FixsR)
we have
\Gamma\vdash(\mathsf{fix}\,f(x).\,M):B_{1}\to B_{2}
.

By
(SubR)
we have
\Gamma,\,\vdash\mathsf{fix}\,f(x).\,M:A
as required.

(FixnR2)
:

Let
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma)\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}(\mathsf{fix}\,f(x).\,M):A
and
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma),\,f:A,\,M:B_{2}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}x:B_{1}
with
B_{1}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}B_{2}\sqsubseteq A
.

By the inductive hypothesis,
\Gamma,\,f:A,\,M:B_{2}\vdash x:B_{1}
.

By
(SubL)
we have
\Gamma,\,f:B_{1}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}B_{2},\,M:B_{2}\vdash x:B_{1}
.

By
(FixnR)
we have
\Gamma\vdash_{2}(\mathsf{fix}\,f(x).\,M):B_{1}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}A_{1}
.

By
(SubR)
we have
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{fix}\,f(x).\,M:A
as required.

(AppR2)
:

Let
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma)\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}(M\ N):\tau_{3}
,
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma)\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}M:\tau_{1}
, and
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma)\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}N:\tau_{2}
with
\tau_{1}\sqsubseteq\tau_{2}\to\tau_{3}
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we have
\Gamma\vdash M:\tau_{1}
and
\Gamma\vdash N:\tau_{2}
.
By
(SubR)
on
M
, we have
\Gamma\vdash M:\tau_{2}\to\tau_{3}
.

By
(AppR)
we have
\Gamma\vdash(M\ N):\tau_{3}
as required.

(AppL2)
:

Let
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma),\,(M\ N):\tau_{3}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}\Delta
,
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma)\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}M:\tau_{1}
, and
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma),\,N:\tau_{2}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}\Delta
with
\tau_{1}\sqsubseteq\tau_{2}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}\tau_{3}
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we have
\Gamma\vdash M:\tau_{1}
and
\Gamma,\,N:\tau\vdash_{2}\Delta
.

By
(SubR)
on
M
we have
\Gamma\vdash M:\tau_{2}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}\tau_{3}
.

By
(AppL)
, we have
\Gamma,\,(M\ N):\tau_{3}\vdash\Delta
as required.

(ConsL2)
:

Let
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma),\,c(M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}):\tau\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}\Delta
and
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma),\,M_{i}:A_{i}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}\Delta
with
\tau\sqsubseteq\Sigma_{d\in\mathcal{C}\backslash\{c\}}(d(\vec{A_{d}}))+c(A_{1},\ldots,A_{n})
.

By the inductive hypothesis,
\Gamma,\,M_{i}:A_{i}\vdash\Delta
.

By
(CnsL)
, we have
\Gamma,\,c(M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}):c(A_{1},\ldots,A_{n})+\Sigma_{d\in\mathcal{C}\backslash\{c\}}(d(\vec{A_{d}}))\vdash\Delta
.

By
(SubL)
, we have
\Gamma,\,c(M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}):\tau\vdash\Delta
as required.

(ConsR2)
:

Let
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma)\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}c(_{1},\ldots,M_{n}):\tau
and
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma)\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}M_{i}:A_{i}\;(\forall i)
with
c(A_{1},\ldots,A_{n})\sqsubseteq\tau
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we have
\Gamma\mid C\vdash M_{i}:A_{i}\;(\forall i)
.

By
(CnsR)
, we have
\Gamma\vdash_{2}c(_{1},\ldots,M_{n}):c(A_{1},\ldots,A_{n})
.

By
(SubR)
, we have
\Gamma\vdash c(_{1},\ldots,M_{n}):\tau
as required.

(MchL2)
:

Let
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma),\,\mathsf{match}\,M\,\mathsf{with}\,\{|_{i=1}^{k}(p_{i}\mapsto P_{i})\}:A\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}\Delta
,
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma),\,(M,P_{i}):A^{\prime}_{i}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}\Delta\;(\forall i)
and
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma),\,P_{i}:A_{x}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}x:B_{x}\;(\forall i.\forall x\in\mathsf{FV}{p_{i}})
where
A\sqsubseteq A_{x}\;(\forall i.\forall x\in\mathsf{FV}{(p_{i})})
,
A\sqsubseteq A_{i}\;(\forall i.)
,
(B_{i},A_{i})\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}_{i}
, and
p_{i}[B_{x}/x\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}{(p_{i})}]\sqsubseteq B_{i}\;(\forall i.)
.

By the inductive hypothesis,
\Gamma,\,(M,P_{i}):A^{\prime}_{i}\vdash\Delta\;(\forall i)
and
\Gamma,\,P_{i}:A_{x}\vdash x:B_{x}\;(\forall i.\forall x\in\mathsf{FV}{p_{i}})
.

By
(SubL)
(applied to
(M,P_{i})
twice and
P_{i}
once), we have
\Gamma,\,(M,P_{i}):(p_{i}[B_{x}/x\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}{(p_{i})}],A)\vdash\Delta\;(\forall i)
and
\Gamma,\,P_{i}:A\vdash x:B_{x}\;(\forall i.\forall x\in\mathsf{FV}{p_{i}})
.

By
(MchL)
, we have
\Gamma,\,\mathsf{match}\,M\,\mathsf{with}\,\{|_{i=1}^{k}(p_{i}\mapsto P_{i})\}:A\vdash\Delta
as required.

(MchR2)
:

Let
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma)\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}\mathsf{match}\,M\,\mathsf{with}\,\{|_{i=1}^{k}(p_{i}\mapsto P_{i})\}:A
,
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma)\cup\{x:B_{x}\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})\}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}P_{i}:A_{i}\;(\forall i)
, and
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma)\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}M:B
where
B\sqsubseteq\Sigma_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}[B_{x}/x\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})]
and
A_{i}\sqsubseteq A\;(\forall i)
.

By the inductive hypothesis we have
\Gamma\cup\{x:B_{x}\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})\}\vdash P_{i}:A_{i}\;(\forall i)
and
\Gamma\vdash M:B
.

By
(SubR)
(applied to
M
and
P_{i}
), we have
\Gamma\cup\{x:B_{x}\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})\}\vdash P_{i}:A\;(\forall i)
and
\Gamma\vdash M:p_{i}[B_{x}/x\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})]
.

By
(MchR)
, we have
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{match}\,M\,\mathsf{with}\,\{|_{i=1}^{k}(p_{i}\mapsto P_{i})\}:A
.

(VarK2)
:

This case is trivial by the use of
(VarK)
.

(CnsK2)
:

This case is trivial by the use of
(CnsK)
.

(FunK2)
:

Let
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma),\,M\ N:A\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma)\vdash_{2}\Delta
,
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma),\,M:B\vdash_{2}\Delta
, and
\mathsf{Ok}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}A\sqsubseteq B
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we have
\Gamma,\,M:B\vdash\Delta
.

By
(SubL)
, we have
\Gamma,\,M:\mathsf{Ok}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}A\vdash\Delta
.

By
(FunK)
, we have
\Gamma,\,M\ N:\mathsf{Ok}\vdash\Delta
.

By
(SubL)
, we have
\Gamma,\,M\ N:A\vdash\Delta
as required.

(CnsDL21)
:

This case is trivial by the use of
(CnsDL)
where the type
A
is an arrow.

(CnsDL22)
:

This case is trivial by the use of
(CnsDL)
where the type
A
is an arrow.

(CnsDL23)
:

This case is trivial by the use of
(CnsDL)
where the type
A
is a sum of constructor types that does not include
c
.

(FixDL2)
:

This case is trivial by the use of
(FixDL)
where the type
A
is a sum of constructor types.

∎

Theorem D.4 (Completeness of Algorithmic Type Assignment).

For all
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta,\Delta^{\prime}
, if the following hold:

(1)

\Gamma\vdash\Delta

(2)

\Delta\sqsubseteq\Delta^{\prime}

(3)

\Gamma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma

Then
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash_{2}\Delta^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the derivation of
\vdash
.

(GVar)
:

This case is trivial by the use of
(Inst2)
.

(Id)
:

Let
\Gamma,\,x:A\vdash x:A
,
\Gamma^{\prime},\,x:C\sqsubseteq\Gamma,\,x:A
, and
A\sqsubseteq B
.

Then by
(Var2)
,
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime}),\,x:C\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma),\,C\sqsubseteq B\vdash_{2}x:B
.

(SubL)
:

Let
\Gamma,\,M:A\vdash\Delta
,
\Gamma,\,M:B\vdash\Delta
, and
A\sqsubseteq B
.

By the inductive hypothesis, for all
\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma,\,M:A
and
\Delta\sqsubseteq\Delta^{\prime}
we have
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash_{2}\Delta^{\prime}
.

As
A\sqsubseteq B
,
M:A
is an included entry in an instance of
\Gamma^{\prime}
.

Thus, for all
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma
,
A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A
, and
\Delta\sqsubseteq\Delta^{\prime\prime}
, we have
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}),\,M:A^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
.

(SubR)
:

Let
\Gamma\vdash M:A
,
\Gamma\vdash M:B
, and
B\sqsubseteq A
.

By the inductive hypothesis, for all
\Gamma^{\prime},B^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma
and
B\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}
we have
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash_{2}M:B^{\prime}
.

Thus, as
B\sqsubseteq A
we have for all
A^{\prime}
such that
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
,
B\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
and so
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash_{2}M:A^{\prime}
as required.

(FixsR)
:

Let
\Gamma\vdash(\mathsf{fix}\,f(x).\,M):B\to A
and
\Gamma,\,f:B\to A,\,x:B\vdash M:A
.

By the inductive hypothesis, for all
\Gamma^{\prime},B^{\prime},A^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma
,
B^{\prime}\sqsubseteq B
, and
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
, we have
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime}),\,f:B^{\prime}\to A^{\prime},\,x:B^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash_{2}M:A^{\prime}
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma
,
B^{\prime}\sqsubseteq B
, and
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
. Then
B\to A\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}\to A^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}),\,f:B^{\prime}\to A^{\prime},\,x:B^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}M:A^{\prime}
.

Then, by
(FixsR2)
, we have
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}\mathsf{fix}\,f(x).\,M:B^{\prime}\to A^{\prime}
as required.

(FixnR)
:

Let
\Gamma\vdash(\mathsf{fix}\,f(x).\,M):B\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}A
and
\Gamma,\,f:B\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}A,\,M:A\vdash x:B
.

By the inductive hypothesis, for all
\Gamma^{\prime},A^{\prime},B^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma
,
B\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}
and
A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A
, we have
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime}),\,f:B^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}A^{\prime},\,M:A^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash_{2}x:B^{\prime}
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma
,
A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A
,
B\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}
. Then
B\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}A\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}A^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}),\,f:B^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}A^{\prime},\,M:A^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}x:B^{\prime}
.

Then, by
(FixnR2)
, we have
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}(\mathsf{fix}\,f(x).\,M):B^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}A^{\prime}
as required.

(AppL)
:

Let
\Gamma,\,(M\ N):A\vdash\Delta
,
\Gamma\vdash M:B\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}A
, and
\Gamma,\,N:B\vdash\Delta
.

By the inductive hypothesis, for all
\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime},A^{\prime},B^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma
,
\Delta\sqsubseteq\Delta^{\prime}
,
B\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}A\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}A^{\prime}
, and
B\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}
we have
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash_{2}M:B^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}A^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime}),\,N:B^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash_{2}\Delta^{\prime}
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma
,
A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A
, and
\Delta\sqsubseteq\Delta^{\prime}
. Then, for all
B^{\prime}
such that
B\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}A\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}A^{\prime}
and
B\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}
, we have
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}M:B^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}A^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}),\,N:B^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
.

Then, by
(AppL2)
, as the side condition is trivially satisfied, we have
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}),\,(M\ N):A^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
.

(AppR)
:

Let
\Gamma\vdash(M\ N):A
,
\Gamma\vdash M:B\to A
, and
\Gamma\vdash N:B
.

By the inductive hypothesis, for all
\Gamma^{\prime},A^{\prime},B^{\prime}
we have
\Gamma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma
,
B\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}
,
B\to A\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}\to A^{\prime}
,
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash_{2}M:B^{\prime}\to A^{\prime}
, and
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash_{2}N:B^{\prime}
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma
and
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
. Then, for all
B^{\prime}
such that
B\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}
and
B\to A\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}\to A^{\prime}
, we have
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}M:B^{\prime}\to A^{\prime}
, and
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}N:B^{\prime}
.

Then, by
(AppR2)
, as the side condition is trivially satisfied, we have
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}(M\ N):A^{\prime}
as required.

(CnsL)
:

Let
\Gamma,\,c(M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}):c(A_{1},\ldots,A_{n})+\Sigma_{d\in\mathcal{C}\backslash\{c\}}(d(\vec{A_{d}}))\vdash\Delta
and
\Gamma,\,M_{i}:A_{i}\vdash\Delta
for some
i
.

By the inductive hypothesis, for all
\Gamma^{\prime},A^{\prime}_{i},\Delta^{\prime}
we have
\Gamma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma
,
\Delta\sqsubseteq\Delta^{\prime}
,
A^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubseteq A_{i}
, and
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime}),\,M_{i}:A^{\prime}_{i}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash_{2}\Delta^{\prime}
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma
,
\Delta\sqsubseteq\Delta^{\prime\prime}
and
A^{\prime}_{j}\sqsubseteq A_{j}(\forall j)
. Then
c(A^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,A^{\prime}_{n})+\Sigma_{d\in\mathcal{C}\backslash\{c\}}(d(\vec{A^{\prime}_{d}}))\sqsubseteq c(A_{1},\ldots,A_{n})+\Sigma_{d\in\mathcal{C}\backslash\{c\}}(d(\vec{A_{d}}))
and
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}),\,M_{i}:A^{\prime}_{i}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
.

Then, by
(ConsL2)
, we have
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}),\,c(M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}):c(A^{\prime}_{i},\ldots,A^{\prime}_{n})+\Sigma_{d\in\mathcal{C}\backslash\{c\}}(d(\vec{A^{\prime}_{d}}))\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
.

(CnsR)
:

Let
\Gamma\vdash c(M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}):c(A_{1},\ldots,A_{n})
and
\Gamma\vdash M_{i}:A_{i}
for all
i
.

By the inductive hypothesis, for all
\Gamma^{\prime},A^{\prime}_{i}
we have
\Gamma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma
,
A_{i}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}_{i}
, and
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash_{2}M_{i}:A^{\prime}_{i}
for all
i
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma
and
A_{i}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}_{i}\;(\forall i)
. Then
c(A_{i},\ldots,A_{n})\sqsubseteq c(A^{\prime}_{i},\ldots,A^{\prime}_{n})
and
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}M_{i}:A^{\prime}_{i}\;(\forall i)
.

Then, by
(ConsR2)
, we have
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}c(M_{i},\ldots,M_{n}):c(A^{\prime}_{i},\ldots,A^{\prime}_{n})
as required.

(MchL)
:

Let
\Gamma,\,\mathsf{match}\,M\,\mathsf{with}\,\{|_{i=1}^{k}(p_{i}\mapsto P_{i})\}:A\vdash\Delta
,
\Gamma,\,P_{i}:A\vdash x:B_{x}\;(\forall i.\forall x\in\mathsf{FV}{(p_{i})})
, and
\Gamma,\,(M,P_{i}):(p_{i}[B_{x}/x\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}{(p_{i})}],A)\vdash\Delta\;(\forall i)
.

By the inductive hypothesis we have:

(1):

\forall\Gamma^{\prime},A_{x},B^{\prime}_{x}.\Gamma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma\wedge A_{x}\sqsubseteq A\wedge B_{x}\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}_{x}\Rightarrow\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime}),\,P_{i}:A_{x}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash_{2}x:B^{\prime}_{x}

(2):

\forall\Gamma^{\prime},\tau,\Delta^{\prime}.\Gamma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma\wedge\tau\sqsubseteq(p_{i}[B_{x}/x\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}{(p_{i})}],A)\wedge\Delta\sqsubseteq\Delta^{\prime}\Rightarrow\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime}),\,(M,P_{i}):(p_{i}[B^{\prime}_{x}/x\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}{(p_{i})}],A^{\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash_{2}\Delta^{\prime}

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma
,
A^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq A
, and
\Delta\sqsubseteq\Delta^{\prime\prime}
.

By instantiating (1):
\Gamma^{\prime}
with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
,
A_{x}
with
A^{\prime\prime}
, and
B^{\prime}_{x}
with
B_{x}
gives
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}),\,P_{i}:A^{\prime\prime}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}x:B_{x}
.

By instantiating (2):
\Gamma^{\prime}
with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
,
\tau
with
(B_{x},A^{\prime\prime})
, and
\Delta^{\prime}
with
\Delta^{\prime\prime}
gives
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}),\,(M,P_{i}):(p_{i}[B_{x}/x\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}{(p_{i})}],A^{\prime\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
.

Finally, by
(MchL2)
, as the side conditions are trivially satisfied, we have
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}),\,\mathsf{match}\,M\,\mathsf{with}\,\{|_{i=1}^{k}(p_{i}\mapsto P_{i})\}:A^{\prime\prime}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
as required.

(MchR)
:

Let
\Gamma\mid C\vdash\mathsf{match}\,M\,\mathsf{with}\,\{|_{i=1}^{k}(p_{i}\mapsto P_{i})\}:A
,
\Gamma\vdash M:\Sigma_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}[B_{x}/x\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})]
, and
\Gamma\cup\{x:B_{x}\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})\}\vdash P_{i}:A(\forall i)
.

By the inductive hypothesis, for all
\Gamma^{\prime},A^{\prime},B,B^{\prime}_{x}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma
,
\Sigma_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}[B_{x}/x\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})]\sqsubseteq B
,
(\forall i)B^{\prime}_{x}\sqsubseteq B_{x}
, and
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
, we have

(1):

\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash_{2}M:B

(2):

\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime})\cup\{x:B^{\prime}_{x}\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})\}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash_{2}P_{i}:A^{\prime}(\forall i)

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma
and
A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}
. We want to show
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}\mathsf{match}\,M\,\mathsf{with}\,\{|_{i=1}^{k}(p_{i}\mapsto P_{i})\}:A^{\prime\prime}
.

Instantiating (1):
\Gamma^{\prime}
with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
and
B
with
\Sigma_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}[B_{x}/x\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})]
gives
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}M:\Sigma_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}[B_{x}/x\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})]
.

Instantiating (2):
\Gamma^{\prime}
with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
,
B^{\prime}_{x}
with
B_{x}
, and
A^{\prime}
with
A^{\prime\prime}
, gives
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\cup\{x:B_{x}\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})\}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}P_{i}:A^{\prime\prime}(\forall i)
.

Finally, by
(MchR2)
, we have
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}\mathsf{match}\,M\,\mathsf{with}\,\{|_{i=1}^{k}(p_{i}\mapsto P_{i})\}:A^{\prime\prime}
as required.

(VarK)
:

This case is trivial by the use of
(VarK2)
.

(CnsK)
:

This case is trivial by the use of
(CnsK2)
with
A
as
\mathsf{Ok}
.

(FunK)
:

Let
\Gamma,\,M\ N:\mathsf{Ok}\vdash\Delta
and
\Gamma,\,M:\mathsf{Ok}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}A\vdash\Delta
.

By the inductive hypothesis, for all
\Gamma^{\prime},B,\Delta^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma^{\prime}
,
B\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}A
, and
\Delta\sqsubseteq\Delta^{\prime}
, we have
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime}),\,M:B\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash_{2}\Delta^{\prime}
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma
and
\Delta^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\Delta^{\prime\prime}
.

By instantiating
\Gamma^{\prime}
with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
,
\Delta^{\prime}
with
\Delta^{\prime\prime}
, and
B
with
\mathsf{Ok}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}\mathsf{Ok}
, we have
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}),\,M:\mathsf{Ok}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}\mathsf{Ok}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
.

By
(FunK2)
, we have
\mathsf{Typ}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}),\,M\ N:\mathsf{Ok}\mid\mathsf{Con}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash_{2}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
as required.

(CnsDL)
:

Let
\Gamma,\,c(M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}):A\vdash\Delta
where
A
is and arrow type or of the shape
\Sigma_{d\in I}
with
c\notin I
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\Gamma
,
A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A
, and
\Delta\sqsubseteq\Delta^{\prime}
.

If
A
is a sum of constructor types that does not include
c
, then so is
A^{\prime}
. Thus, by
(CnsDL23)
, we have
\Gamma^{\prime},\,c(M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}):A^{\prime}\vdash\Delta^{\prime}
.

If
A
is a sufficiency arrow (
\tau_{1}\to\tau_{2}
), then so is
A^{\prime}
. Thus, by
(CnsDL21)
, we have
\Gamma^{\prime},\,c(M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}):A^{\prime}\vdash\Delta^{\prime}
.

If
A
is a necessity arrow (
\tau_{1}\mathrel{\text{\stixarr\char 179\relax}}\tau_{2}
), then so is
A^{\prime}
. Thus, by
(CnsDL22)
, we have
\Gamma^{\prime},\,c(M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}):A^{\prime}\vdash\Delta^{\prime}
.

∎

Figure 11
.
Type Inference Algorithm - InferL

Theorem D.5 (Soundness of the Inference Algorithm).

Let
\Gamma
and
\Delta
be strongly consistent variable environments,
M
be a term,
A
be a type,
\sigma
be a substitution from type variables to types, and
C,C^{\prime}
be sets of constraints. Then:

(1)

(\Gamma,\,M:A\mid C\vdash\Delta)\in
InferL(
\Gamma
,
M
,
\Delta
)
\Rightarrow

\forall\sigma.(\Gamma\sigma,\,M:A\sigma\mid C\sigma\vdash_{2}\Delta\sigma)

(2)

(\Gamma\mid C\vdash M:A)\in
InferR(
\Gamma
,
M
)
\Rightarrow

\forall\sigma.(\Gamma\sigma\mid C\sigma\vdash_{2}M:A\sigma)

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the shape of the term
M
.

Specially treated rule:

In
InferL
there is a specially generated set that does not fall inline nicely with the others. The section of interest is:

⬇

{

\Gamma

\cup

{
M

:

a
}

|

{
Ok

\sqsubseteq

b
}

\vdash

\Delta

|

a

=

freshVar
,

(
x

:

b
)

\in

\Delta

}

This set is the judgment(s) provable by
(VarK2)
. This case is proven trivially but it is important to note that this judgment is produced by all terms
M
if the delta of the judgment is a variable. As this case is trivially sound, it will be ignored in future cases despite being generated in all of them.

The second set creates a judgment for every typing of the variable at a (constrained) type scheme.

Fix a generated judgment,

Let

be a type variable substitution and

.

Then, by
(Inst2)
, we have
\Gamma\sigma\mid C^{\prime}\sigma\vdash_{2}x:a^{\prime}\sigma
.

The first set generates judgments for the
(Var2)
and
(VarK2)
rules.

Let

be a type variable substitution.

By

\Gamma\sigma,\,x:t\sigma\mid\{t\sigma\sqsubseteq b\sigma\}\vdash_{2}x:b\sigma

If

, we have
\Gamma\sigma\mid\{\mathsf{Ok}\sqsubseteq b\sigma\}\vdash_{2}x:b

Let

\Gamma\sigma,\,x:a\sigma\mid\{a\sigma\sqsubseteq b\sigma\}\vdash_{2}x:b\sigma

})),

c

=

\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}(c_{i}\cup\{a\sqsubseteq a_{i},(a_{i},b_{i})\sqsubseteq a^{\prime}_{i},p_{i}[b_{(i,x)}/x\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}{(p_{i})}]\sqsubseteq b_{i}\})

\cup

\bigcup_{(i,x)\in[1..k]\times\mathsf{FV}{(p_{i})}}(c^{\prime}_{(i,x)}\cup\{a\sqsubseteq a_{(i,x)}\})

}

By the inductive hypothesis (applied to lines 5 and 6), we have:

(1):

\forall 1\leq i\leq k.\forall\sigma.\Gamma\sigma,\,(Q,P_{i}):a^{\prime}_{i}\sigma\mid C_{i}\sigma\vdash_{2}\Delta\sigma

(2):

\forall 1\leq i\leq k.\forall x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i}).\forall\sigma.\Gamma\sigma,\,P_{i}:a_{(i,x)}\sigma\mid C^{\prime}_{(i,x)}\sigma\vdash_{2}\Delta\sigma

Let
\sigma
be a type variable substitution and constraint set
C
be defined by:

\displaystyle C:=

\displaystyle\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}(C_{i}\cup\{a\sqsubseteq a_{i},(a_{i},b_{i})\sqsubseteq a^{\prime}_{i},p_{i}[b_{(i,x)}/x\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}{(p_{i})}]\sqsubseteq b_{i}\})\cup

\displaystyle\bigcup_{(i,x)\in[1..k]\times\mathsf{FV}{(p_{i})}}(C^{\prime}_{(i,x)}\cup\{a\sqsubseteq a_{(i,x)}\})

Then, for all combinations of typings, by
(MchL2)
, we have
\Gamma\sigma,\,M:a\sigma\mid C\sigma\vdash_{2}\Delta\sigma
.

∎

Theorem D.6 (Completeness of the Inference Algorithm).

Let
\tau,\sigma
be substitutions from type variables to types such that
\sigma
extends
\tau
,
\Gamma\tau,\Delta\tau
be strongly consistent variable environments,
M
be a term,
A
be a type, and
C,C^{\prime}
be sets of constraints. Then:

(1)

\Gamma\tau,\,M:A\mid C\vdash_{2}\Delta\tau

\Rightarrow

\exists\sigma.\exists(\Gamma,\,M:A^{\prime}\mid C^{\prime}\vdash\Delta)\in
InferL(
\Gamma
,
M
,
\Delta
).
(A=A^{\prime}\sigma)\wedge(C\vdash C^{\prime}\sigma)

(2)

\Gamma\tau\mid C\vdash_{2}M:A

\Rightarrow

\exists\sigma.\exists(\Gamma\mid C^{\prime}\vdash M:A^{\prime})\in
InferR(
\Gamma
,
M
).
(A=A^{\prime}\sigma)\wedge(C\vdash C^{\prime}\sigma)

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the derivation of
\vdash_{2}
.

(Inst2)
:

For some
\tau
, we have
\Gamma\tau,\,f:\forall\vec{a}.C^{\prime}\Rightarrow A\mid C\vdash_{2}f:A^{\prime}
,

.

⬇

:

\in

=

\overline{\text{freshVar}}

freshVar

Let
\sigma
be a type variable substitution defined by

C\vdash(C^{\prime}_{i}\cup\{Ok\sqsubseteq b\})\sigma
.

(MchR2)
:

For some
\tau
, we have:

(1):

\Gamma\tau\mid C\vdash_{2}\mathsf{match}\,Q\,\mathsf{with}\,\{|_{i=1}^{k}(p_{i}\mapsto P_{i})\}:A

(2):

\gamma\tau\mid C\vdash_{2}Q:B

(3):

\Gamma\tau\cup\{x:B_{x}\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}{(p_{i})}\}\mid C\vdash_{2}P_{i}:A_{i}\;(\forall i)

(4):

C\vdash B\sqsubseteq\Sigma_{i=1}^{k}(p_{i}[B_{x}/x\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})])

(5):

\forall i.C\vdash A_{i}\sqsubseteq A

By the inductive hypothesis, we have:

(1):

\exists\sigma_{0}.\exists(\Gamma\mid C^{\prime}_{0}\vdash Q:b)\in\text{InferR}(\Gamma,\,Q).B=b\sigma_{0}\wedge C\vdash C^{\prime}_{0}\sigma_{0}

(2):

\forall 1\leq i\leq k.\exists\sigma_{i}.\exists(\Gamma\cup\{x:B_{x}\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})\}\mid C^{\prime}_{i}\vdash P_{i}:a_{i})\in\text{InferR}(\Gamma\cup\{x:B_{x}\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})\},\,P_{i}).A_{i}=a_{i}\sigma_{i}\wedge C\vdash C^{\prime}_{i}\sigma_{2}

Observe this extract from
InferR
in the match case:

⬇

{

|

By choosing the correct inferred judgment(s) in lines 4 and 5 makes it clear that the correct witness judgment can be inferred.

Defining the witness
\sigma:=\sigma_{0}\cup\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}(\sigma_{i}\cup[(b_{x})_{x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})}\mapsto(B_{x})_{x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})}])\cup[a\mapsto A]
satisfies the requirements of
A=a\sigma
and
C\vdash(\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}(C^{\prime}_{i}\cup\{b\sqsubseteq p_{i}[b_{x}/x\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})],a_{i}\sqsubseteq a\})\cup C^{\prime}_{0})\sigma
.

(MchL2)
:

For some
\tau
, we have:

(1):

\Gamma\tau,\,\mathsf{match}\,Q\,\mathsf{with}\,\{|_{i=1}^{k}(p_{i}\mapsto P_{i})\}:A\mid C\vdash_{2}\Delta\tau

(2):

\forall i.\Gamma\tau,\,(M,P_{i}):A^{\prime}_{i}\mid C\vdash_{2}\Delta\tau

(3):

\forall i.\forall x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i}).\Gamma,\,P_{i}:A_{x}\mid C\vdash_{2}x:B_{x}

(4):

\forall i.C\vdash A\sqsubseteq A_{i}

(5):

\forall i.C\vdash(B_{i},A_{i})\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}_{i}

(6):

\forall i.\forall x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i}).C\vdash A\sqsubseteq A_{x}

(7):

\forall i.C\vdash p_{i}[B_{x}/x\mid x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})]\sqsubseteq B_{i}

By the inductive hypothesis, we have:

(1):

\forall i.\exists\sigma^{\prime}_{i}.\exists(\Gamma,\,(M,P_{i}):a^{\prime}_{i}\mid C^{\prime}_{i}\vdash\Delta)\in\text{InferL}(\Gamma,\,(M,P_{i}),\,\Delta).A^{\prime}_{i}=a^{\prime}_{i}\sigma^{\prime}_{i}\wedge C\vdash C^{\prime}_{i}\sigma^{\prime}_{i}

(2):

\forall i.\forall x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i}).\exists\sigma_{(i,x)}.\exists(\Gamma,\,P_{i}:a_{x}\mid C_{(i,x)}\vdash x:b_{x})\in\text{InferL}(\Gamma,\,P_{i},\,\{x:b_{x}\}).A_{x}=a_{x}\sigma_{(i,x)}\wedge C\vdash C_{(i,x)}\sigma_{(i,x)}

Observe this extract from
InferL
in the match case:

⬇

{

\cup

{

:

}

|

\vdash

|

=

,

=

,

=

\overline{\text{freshVar}}
,

(

\cup

{(

,

P_{i}
)

:

}

)

(

(

(

\cup

\bigcup_{(i,x)\in[1..k]\times\mathsf{FV}{(p_{i})}}(c^{\prime}_{(i,x)}\cup\{a\sqsubseteq a_{(i,x)}\})
